# Aryna Sabalenka

In this name that follows [East Slavic naming customs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Slavic_naming_customs), the [patronymic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patronymic) is&#x20;*&#x20;Siarhiejeŭna* and the [family name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surname) is&#x20;*&#x20;Sabalenka*.

**Aryna Siarhiejeŭna Sabalenka**[^cite_note-translit-3] (born 5 May 1998) is a Belarusian professional [tennis](29773.md) player.[^cite_note-WTA25-1] She has been ranked the world No. 1 in women's [singles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_WTA_number_1_ranked_singles_tennis_players) by the [Women's Tennis Association](57768.md) (WTA) for a total of 107 weeks ([tenth-most of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_WTA_number_1_ranked_singles_tennis_players#Weeks_at_No._1)), and finished as the [year-end No. 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_WTA_number_1_ranked_singles_tennis_players#Year-end_No._1_player) twice ([2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_WTA_Tour), [2025](75429018.md)). Sabalenka has won 24 career singles titles, including two titles at the [Australian Open](97851.md) and two at the [US Open](82729.md), as well as eleven [WTA 1000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTA_1000) events. She is also a former [world No. 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_WTA_number_1_ranked_doubles_tennis_players) in women's doubles and has won six doubles titles, including the [2019 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_doubles) and [2021 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_doubles), both with [Elise Mertens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elise_Mertens).

Sabalenka came to prominence in 2017 when she and [Aliaksandra Sasnovich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aliaksandra_Sasnovich) led the [Belarus Fed Cup team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarus_Fed_Cup_team) to a runner-up finish despite both being ranked outside the top 75 at the time. She finished 2018 and 2019 ranked world No. 11 in singles. Following two major singles semifinal appearances in 2021, Sabalenka rose to world No. 2 but struggled to maintain that success in 2022 consistently. In 2023, she won her first major singles title at the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), finished runner-up at the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), and obtained the world No. 1 ranking for the first time.

In 2024, Sabalenka defended her [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) title and won a third major singles title at the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), finishing as the year-end No. 1 for the first time. In 2025, she defended her title at the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) and was runner-up at the [Australian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) and [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) Opens, holding the No. 1 position for the entire season. In 2026, she reached two major singles finals ([Australian Open](81715708.md) and [US Open](84044135.md)) and completed the [Sunshine Double](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunshine_Double) (winning [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) and [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) back-to-back). Also in 2026, Sabalenka won the [Laureus World Sports Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laureus_World_Sports_Award) for [Sportswoman of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laureus_World_Sports_Award_for_Sportswoman_of_the_Year).

&#x20;Sabalenka began playing doubles regularly in 2019. With Mertens as her partner, she found instant success by winning the [Sunshine Double](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunshine_Double) ([Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_doubles) and [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_doubles)) that same year. They won the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_doubles) later in the season, and Sabalenka qualified for the [WTA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_WTA_Finals_%E2%80%93_Doubles) for the first time. After she and Mertens won their second major title at the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_doubles) in 2021, Sabalenka became the doubles world No. 1, after which she shifted to playing exclusively singles.

## Early life and background

Sabalenka was born in [Minsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minsk), the capital of [Belarus](3457.md). Her father, Sergey (died 2019), was an [ice hockey](14790.md) player. Sabalenka started playing tennis by chance. She said, "One day, my dad was just driving me somewhere in the car, and he saw tennis courts on the way. So he took me to the courts. I liked it and enjoyed it, and that's how it was. That's how it started." She began training at the National Tennis Academy in Minsk when it opened in 2014.[^cite_note-first-post-4][^cite_note-tut-2017-5][^cite_note-6] In 2015, the Belarusian Tennis Federation persuaded Sabalenka and her team to focus on playing low-level professional events instead of junior tournaments, even though she was still eligible to compete at the junior level.[^cite_note-tut-2016-7]

## Career

### Juniors

Sabalenka had a late start on the [ITF Junior Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ITF_Junior_Circuit), instead competing on the U14 and U16 Tennis Europe tours at a younger age.[^cite_note-tennis-is-world-8][^cite_note-tennis-europe-9][^cite_note-tennis-africa-latest-10] She did not compete in the main draw of any ITF events until 2013 at the low-level Grade-4 Tallink Cup in Estonia at the age of 15. She never played in the junior Grand Slam tournaments or any other high-level Grade-A and Grade-1 events. Without the higher point levels from these bigger tournaments, she had a career-high ranking of just No. 225.[^cite_note-SabItfJrOvr1-11][^cite_note-note1-12] Sabalenka won her first ITF title in doubles at the lowest-level Grade-5 Alatan Tour Cup in Belarus in late 2013 with compatriot [Vera Lapko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vera_Lapko) as her partner.[^cite_note-SabItfJrAtv2-13][^cite_note-note1-12] In 2014, she excelled at Grade 4 events. She reached her first singles final at the Estonian Junior Open in June and won her first singles title at the MTV Total Junior Cup in Finland in October.[^cite_note-SabItfJrAtv1-14][^cite_note-note1-12] At the end of the season, Sabalenka defended her Alatan Tour Cup doubles title, this time with compatriot Nika Shytkouskaya,[^cite_note-SabItfJrAtv2-13][^cite_note-note1-12] and also won the singles title.[^cite_note-SabItfJrAtv1-14][^cite_note-note1-12] She only played in one tournament in 2015, the European Junior Championships. As a Grade B1 event, this was the highest-level junior tournament she played in. She lost in the second round to top seed [Markéta Vondroušová](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark%C3%A9ta_Vondrou%C5%A1ov%C3%A1).[^cite_note-tennis-is-world-8][^cite_note-SabItfJrOvr1-11][^cite_note-note1-12]

### 2012–2016: Professional & Fed Cup debut

Sabalenka began playing on the [ITF Women's Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ITF_Women's_World_Tennis_Tour) in 2012, even before she competed on the [ITF Junior Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ITF_Junior_Circuit). Her first five tournaments were in her hometown of Minsk and spread out over two years, but she did not win a main draw match in any of them. She won her first professional match at the very end of 2014 in Istanbul.[^cite_note-SabItfSrAtv1-15][^cite_note-note1-12] The following season in October, she won her first two titles in back-to-back weeks in [Antalya](361527.md), both at the \$10k level. Sabalenka also won a \$25k title the last week of the year.[^cite_note-SabItfSrAtv1-15][^cite_note-note1-12] This title put her into the top 300 WTA rankings for the first time at the start of 2016.[^cite_note-SabWtaRnk-16] That year, she made her Fed Cup debut in April, losing her only match.[^cite_note-fed-cup-debut-17] She also won her two biggest titles to date at the \$50k level. The first in [Tianjin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Tianjin_Health_Industry_Park)[^cite_note-tianjin-16-18] put her into the top 200 in May and the second in [Toyota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Dunlop_World_Challenge)[^cite_note-19] in November helped her finish the year ranked at No. 137 in the world.[^cite_note-SabItfSrAtv1-15][^cite_note-note1-12][^cite_note-SabWtaRnk-16]

### 2017: WTA 125 title, top 100



Sabalenka at the [2017 Washington Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Citi_Open)

Despite some early season success in the Fed Cup, Sabalenka had a quiet start to the year. She played in her first WTA Tour main draw in February as a qualifier at the [Dubai Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Dubai_Tennis_Championships);[^cite_note-2017-year-in-review-20] however, she did not win her first WTA Tour match until [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Wimbledon_Championships) in July. In her Grand Slam debut, she again reached the main draw through qualifying and defeated [Irina Khromacheva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irina_Khromacheva) in the opening round.[^cite_note-21] Sabalenka followed up this achievement with another win at the [Washington Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Citi_Open) over No. 34 [Lauren Davis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lauren_Davis), the 2016 runner-up and the highest-ranked player she had defeated at the time.[^cite_note-davis-win-22]

After losing in qualifying at the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_US_Open_\(tennis\)), Sabalenka reached her first ever WTA semifinal at the [Tashkent Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Tashkent_Open), defeating third seed and world No. 53, [Tatjana Maria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatjana_Maria), along the way.[^cite_note-23][^cite_note-24] A few weeks later, she entered the [Tianjin Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Tianjin_Open) as the 119th-ranked player in the world, but managed to reach her first WTA tournament final.[^cite_note-25] There, she faced her childhood idol [Maria Sharapova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Sharapova), but ultimately lost in two tight sets. With this performance, she rose to No. 76 in the rankings, entering the top 100 for the first time.[^cite_note-sharapova-final-26][^cite_note-SabWtaRnk-16] After losing a tight Fed Cup final to the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Fed_Cup_team),[^cite_note-fed-cup-final-27] Sabalenka finished the season by winning the biggest title of her career at the time at the [Mumbai Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Mumbai_Open), a [WTA 125](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTA_125_tournaments) event.[^cite_note-28] The title cemented her at No. 78 at the end of the year.[^cite_note-29]

### 2018: Premier 5 title, top 20



Sabalenka at the [2018 Wimbledon Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Wimbledon_Championships)

After playing relatively few WTA events in 2017, Sabalenka used her higher ranking to play exclusively on the WTA Tour 2018.[^cite_note-core-tennis-30] She reached two quarterfinals to begin the year. At the [Shenzhen Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Shenzhen_Longhua_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated [Monica Niculescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monica_Niculescu) and qualifier [Danka Kovinić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danka_Kovini%C4%87) to reach the quarterfinals, where she lost to world No. 1 [Simona Halep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simona_Halep) in straight sets.[^cite_note-shenzhen-qf-31] At the [Hobart International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Hobart_International_%E2%80%93_Singles), she defeated wildcard entrant [Eugenie Bouchard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugenie_Bouchard) and [Zhang Shuai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhang_Shuai) to reach the quarterfinals, where she lost to [Lesia Tsurenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lesia_Tsurenko) in straight sets.[^cite_note-hobart-qf-32] At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), however, she lost her opening-round match to No. 17 [Ashleigh Barty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashleigh_Barty).[^cite_note-grunting-33] At the [Indian Wells Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated [Varvara Lepchenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varvara_Lepchenko) and No. 18 [Svetlana Kuznetsova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Svetlana_Kuznetsova)[^cite_note-34][^cite_note-core-tennis-30] before losing to [Markéta Vondroušová](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark%C3%A9ta_Vondrou%C5%A1ov%C3%A1).

Sabalenka began the clay-court season at the [Ladies Open Lugano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Ladies_Open_Lugano_%E2%80%93_Singles). She defeated [Mihaela Buzărnescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mihaela_Buz%C4%83rnescu), [Polona Hercog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polona_Hercog), [Camila Giorgi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camila_Giorgi), and wildcard entrant [Stefanie Vögele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanie_V%C3%B6gele) to reach her second career final, where she lost to No. 20 [Elise Mertens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elise_Mertens) in straight sets.[^cite_note-35] As a result, her ranking rose to the top 50.[^cite_note-36] At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), however, she lost in the first round to No. 22 [Kiki Bertens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiki_Bertens).[^cite_note-wta-matches-37]

Sabalenka had stronger results on grass. At the [Libéma Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Lib%C3%A9ma_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated Markéta Vondroušová and [Ajla Tomljanović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajla_Tomljanovi%C4%87) to make it to the quarterfinals, where she lost to [Kirsten Flipkens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirsten_Flipkens) in straight sets. At the [Eastbourne International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Eastbourne_International_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated No. 13 [Julia Görges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julia_G%C3%B6rges) and No. 15 Elise Mertens to reach the quarterfinals. In the quarterfinals, she upset No. 7 and defending champion [Karolína Plíšková](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karol%C3%ADna_Pl%C3%AD%C5%A1kov%C3%A1) for her first victory over a top 10 player.[^cite_note-38] She defeated [Agnieszka Radwańska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnieszka_Radwa%C5%84ska) to reach her first WTA Premier final,[^cite_note-39] where she lost to world No. 2 [Caroline Wozniacki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caroline_Wozniacki) in straight sets.[^cite_note-eastbourne-final-40] As a result, her ranking climbed to No. 32.[^cite_note-41] At [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), however, she lost in the first round to No. 28 Mihaela Buzărnescu.[^cite_note-42]

During the North American hardcourt summer season, Sabalenka rose through the rankings.[^cite_note-SabWtaRnk-16] At the [Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Rogers_Cup_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she stunned world No. 2 Caroline Wozniacki for the biggest win of her career,[^cite_note-wozniacki-win-43] but lost in the third round to No. 15 Elise Mertens.[^cite_note-44] At the [Cincinnati Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Western_&_Southern_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka recorded two more top-ten wins over No. 8 Karolína Plíšková[^cite_note-45] and No. 5 [Caroline Garcia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caroline_Garcia) to reach her first Premier 5 quarterfinal.[^cite_note-46] She stunned No. 13 [Madison Keys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madison_Keys) to reach her first Premier 5 semifinal, where she lost to world No. 1 Simona Halep in straight sets.[^cite_note-47] At the [Connecticut Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Connecticut_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), Sabalenka defeated lucky loser [Samantha Stosur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samantha_Stosur), No. 24 [Daria Saville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daria_Saville), lucky loser [Belinda Bencic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belinda_Bencic), and No. 9 Julia Görges to reach the final.[^cite_note-48] In the final, she defeated [Carla Suárez Navarro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carla_Su%C3%A1rez_Navarro) in straight sets to claim her first Premier-tier title[^cite_note-49] and jump into the top 20.[^cite_note-50] Sabalenka closed out this part of the season with her best result at a Grand Slam tournament to date. At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated [Danielle Collins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danielle_Collins), qualifier [Vera Zvonareva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vera_Zvonareva), and No. 5 [Petra Kvitová](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petra_Kvitov%C3%A1) to make it to the fourth round,[^cite_note-kvitova-us-open-win-51] where she lost to No. 19 and eventual champion [Naomi Osaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naomi_Osaka) in three sets.[^cite_note-osaka-us-open-52] She was the only player to win a set against Osaka in the tournament.

At the [Wuhan Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Wuhan_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), Sabalenka defeated Carla Suárez Navarro in the first round. In the second round, she upset No. 6 [Elina Svitolina](27630639.md).[^cite_note-53] She defeated qualifier [Sofia Kenin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sofia_Kenin) and [Dominika Cibulková](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominika_Cibulkov%C3%A1) to reach her second Premier 5 semifinal.[^cite_note-54] She defeated No. 17 Ashleigh Barty to reach her first Premier 5 final.[^cite_note-55] In the final, she defeated [Anett Kontaveit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anett_Kontaveit) in straight sets to win her first Premier 5 title.[^cite_note-56] At the [China Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_China_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated No. 15 [Garbiñe Muguruza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garbi%C3%B1e_Muguruza) and No. 4 Caroline Garcia to reach the quarterfinals,[^cite_note-57] where she lost to wildcard entrant [Wang Qiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Qiang_\(tennis\)) in straight sets.[^cite_note-58] The success in China helped her reach a new career-high singles ranking of No. 11.[^cite_note-newcomer-59] At the end of the season, Sabalenka qualified for the [WTA Elite Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_WTA_Elite_Trophy_%E2%80%93_Singles). She defeated Ashleigh Barty but lost to Caroline Garcia in the group stage.[^cite_note-60] Barty, having defeated Garcia with fewer games lost, advanced out of the group to end Sabalenka's season.[^cite_note-61] Sabalenka was named the [WTA Newcomer of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTA_Newcomer_of_the_Year).[^cite_note-newcomer-59]

### 2019: Premier 5 title, singles top 10, doubles No. 2

#### Singles: Elite Trophy, three titles

Sabalenka began the season at the [Shenzhen Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_WTA_Shenzhen_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles). She defeated Tatjana Maria and [Ekaterina Alexandrova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ekaterina_Alexandrova) to reach the quarterfinals. She reached the semifinals as No. 29 Maria Sharapova retired.[^cite_note-62] She defeated [Wang Yafan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Yafan) to reach the final. In the final, she came from a set down to defeated [Alison Riske](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alison_Riske) and win the title.[^cite_note-63] However, she could not build on this success in the rest of the first half of the year. At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated qualifier [Anna Kalinskaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_Kalinskaya) and [Katie Boulter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katie_Boulter)[^cite_note-64] before losing to [Amanda Anisimova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amanda_Anisimova).[^cite_note-65] Nonetheless, she made her top-10 debut following the event.[^cite_note-66] At the [St. Petersburg Ladies Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_St._Petersburg_Ladies'_Trophy_%E2%80%93_Singles), Sabalenka defeated [Alison Van Uytvanck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alison_Van_Uytvanck) and qualifier Ekaterina Alexandrova to reach the semifinals, where she lost to No. 8 Kiki Bertens in straight sets.[^cite_note-67] At the [Dubai Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Dubai_Tennis_Championships_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated qualifier [Ivana Jorović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivana_Jorovi%C4%87) before losing to Belinda Bencic.[^cite_note-68] At the [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated Ajla Tomljanović and No. 28 Lesia Tsurenko[^cite_note-69] before losing to No. 8 [Angelique Kerber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angelique_Kerber).[^cite_note-70] Following early exits in [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), [Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles),[^cite_note-71] and [Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka reached the semifinals at the [Internationaux de Strasbourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Internationaux_de_Strasbourg_%E2%80%93_Singles), where she lost to [Dayana Yastremska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dayana_Yastremska) in straight sets. At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she lost to Amanda Anisimova again in the second round.[^cite_note-WTAAnisimova-72] At the [Eastbourne International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Eastbourne_International_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated No. 14 and defending champion Caroline Wozniacki to reach the quarterfinals, where she lost to No. 4 Kiki Bertens in three sets.[^cite_note-73] At [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she lost in the first round to [Magdaléna Rybáriková](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magdal%C3%A9na_Ryb%C3%A1rikov%C3%A1).[^cite_note-74]

Sabalenka had a better second half of the season. At the [Silicon Valley Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Silicon_Valley_Classic_%E2%80%93_Singles), she defeated wildcard entrant [CoCo Vandeweghe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CoCo_Vandeweghe), No. 29 Carla Suárez Navarro,[^cite_note-75] and No. 25 [Donna Vekić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donna_Veki%C4%87) to reach the final,[^cite_note-76] where she lost to [Zheng Saisai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zheng_Saisai) in straight sets.[^cite_note-77] At the [Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Rogers_Cup_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she lost in the first round to [Anastasia Pavlyuchenkova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anastasia_Pavlyuchenkova). At the [Cincinnati Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Western_&_Southern_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated [Petra Martić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petra_Marti%C4%87)[^cite_note-78] and Zheng Saisai before losing to [Maria Sakkari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Sakkari). At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated [Victoria Azarenka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Azarenka) before losing to [Yulia Putintseva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yulia_Putintseva).[^cite_note-79] She returned to China following the US Open, and produced stronger results. At the [Zhengzhou Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Zhengzhou_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), Sabalenka defeated [Jeļena Ostapenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Je%C4%BCena_Ostapenko) to reach the quarterfinals, where she lost to No. 23 Petra Martić in three sets. At the [Wuhan Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Wuhan_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), Sabalenka defeated [Aliaksandra Sasnovich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aliaksandra_Sasnovich),[^cite_note-80] Danielle Collins, and No. 8 Kiki Bertens to reach the quarterfinals, without dropping a set. She defeated wildcard entrant [Elena Rybakina](55541443.md) in three sets to reach the semifinals. She upset world No. 1 Ashleigh Barty in straight sets to reach her second straight Wuhan Open final.[^cite_note-81] In the final, she defeated Alison Riske in three sets to defend her title.[^cite_note-82] At the end of the season, Sabalenka qualified for the [WTA Elite Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_WTA_Elite_Trophy_%E2%80%93_Singles) for the second consecutive year. In her round-robin group, she swept Maria Sakkari and Elise Mertens.[^cite_note-83] In the semifinals, she defeated [Karolína Muchová](55266410.md). In the final, she defeated Kiki Bertens in straight sets to take her third title of the year in China.[^cite_note-84] She finished the year for a ranking of world No. 11, the same as she ended 2018.[^cite_note-85]

#### Doubles: US Open title, Sunshine Double



Mertens and Sabalenka at the 2019 French Open

Sabalenka started the year ranked No. 73 in doubles.[^cite_note-SabWtaRnk-16] She began partnering with Elise Mertens in January, when the pair lost to top seeds [Barbora Krejčíková](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbora_Krej%C4%8D%C3%ADkov%C3%A1) and [Kateřina Siniaková](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kate%C5%99ina_Siniakov%C3%A1) in the third round of the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Australian_Open). They had their breakthrough in March at the two Premier Mandatory tournaments. In only their second tournament together, Sabalenka and Mertens won the [Indian Wells Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Indian_Wells_Masters). They defeated three of the top five seeds in the event, including second seeds [Tímea Babos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%ADmea_Babos) and [Kristina Mladenovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kristina_Mladenovic) in the first round and top seeds Krejčíková and Siniaková in the final.[^cite_note-86] The pair matched this success at their next event by winning the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Miami_Open) to complete the Sunshine Double. They defeated three of the top six seeds, including third seeds [Hsieh Su-wei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hsieh_Su-wei) and [Barbora Strýcová](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbora_Str%C3%BDcov%C3%A1) in the second round. They won the final against sixth seeds Samantha Stosur and Zhang Shuai.[^cite_note-87] With these two titles, Sabalenka rose to No. 21 in the world.[^cite_note-SabWtaRnk-16]

Sabalenka and Mertens continued to partner together throughout the year, entering eleven events before the year-end championships. Sabalenka did not have much success in the Grand Slam singles events, but she produced much better results in doubles. Sabalenka and Mertens reached the semifinals at the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_French_Open), losing to second seeds Babos and Mladenovic.[^cite_note-88] They then reached the quarterfinals at [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Wimbledon_Championships), losing to third seeds Hsieh and Strýcová.[^cite_note-89] These were Sabalenka's first two appearances in at least the quarterfinals at a Grand Slam event. Sabalenka and Mertens had their best result of the year at the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_US_Open_\(tennis\)). As the fourth seed at the event, they made it to the final without playing another top-ten-seeded team. In the final, they faced eighth seeds Victoria Azarenka and Ashleigh Barty, the latter of whom had won the title a year earlier with CoCo Vandeweghe. Sabalenka and Mertens defeated Azarenka and Barty in straight sets for their first Grand Slam title in any discipline.[^cite_note-90] With this title, Sabalenka made her top 10 debut in doubles at No. 6 in the world.[^cite_note-SabWtaRnk-16]

Sabalenka and Mertens made one more final during the year, finishing runner-up at the [Wuhan Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Wuhan_Open), where Sabalenka won the singles title.[^cite_note-91] Their three big titles helped them win the Race to Shenzhen and qualify for the [WTA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_WTA_Finals) as the top seeds. Before the event, Sabalenka and Mertens moved up to No. 2 and No. 3 in the rankings, respectively, behind only world No. 1, Barbora Strýcová.[^cite_note-SabWtaRnk-16] At the WTA Finals, the pair were placed in a round robin group with third seeds Babos and Mladenovic, fifth seeds [Chan Hao-ching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chan_Hao-ching) and [Latisha Chan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latisha_Chan), as well as eighth seeds [Anna-Lena Grönefeld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna-Lena_Gr%C3%B6nefeld) and [Demi Schuurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demi_Schuurs). In their opening match, they were upset by Grönefeld and Schuurs in a match tiebreak.[^cite_note-92] After defeating the Chan sisters, Sabalenka and Mertens lost to Babos and Mladenovic in another match tiebreak. They did not advance out of their group.[^cite_note-93]

### 2020: Premier 5 title

Sabalenka started the season at the [Shenzhen Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_WTA_Shenzhen_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles). She defeated qualifier [Margarita Gasparyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margarita_Betova)[^cite_note-94] before losing to [Kristýna Plíšková](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krist%C3%BDna_Pl%C3%AD%C5%A1kov%C3%A1).[^cite_note-95] At the [Adelaide International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Adelaide_International_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka upset No. 4 Simona Halep to reach the semifinals,[^cite_note-96] where she lost to Dayana Yastremska in straight sets.[^cite_note-97] At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she lost in the first round to Carla Suárez Navarro.[^cite_note-98] She enjoyed moderate success in [doubles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_doubles), reaching the quarterfinals with Elise Mertens before losing to the Chan sisters. At the [Dubai Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Dubai_Tennis_Championships_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated Maria Sakkari and Elise Mertens to reach the quarterfinals, where she lost to No. 2 Simona Halep in three sets.[^cite_note-99] At the [Qatar Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Qatar_Total_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), Sabalenka defeated Anett Kontaveit, No. 20 Maria Sakkari, Zheng Saisai[^cite_note-100] and Svetlana Kuznetsova[^cite_note-101] to reach the final. In the final, she defeated No. 11 Petra Kvitová in straight sets to win her third Premier 5 title.[^cite_note-102]

After the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md) shut down the WTA Tour, Sabalenka entered the [Top Seed Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Top_Seed_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles) in August. She defeated [Madison Brengle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madison_Brengle)[^cite_note-103] before falling to [Coco Gauff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coco_Gauff).[^cite_note-104] In [Cincinnati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Western_&_Southern_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated qualifier [CiCi Bellis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CiCi_Bellis) before losing to qualifier [Jessica Pegula](32944701.md). At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated [Océane Dodin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oc%C3%A9ane_Dodin) before losing to Victoria Azarenka.[^cite_note-105] She had moderate success in doubles, reaching the quarterfinals of both events. Her results began to improve on clay. At the [Internationaux de Strasbourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Internationaux_de_Strasbourg_%E2%80%93_Singles), Sabalenka defeated qualifier [Ellen Perez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ellen_Perez), [Anna Blinkova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_Blinkova), and [Kateřina Siniaková](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kate%C5%99ina_Siniakov%C3%A1) to reach the semifinals, where she lost to No. 5 Elina Svitolina in three sets.[^cite_note-106] At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated Jessica Pegula and [Daria Kasatkina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daria_Kasatkina) before losing to [Ons Jabeur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ons_Jabeur).[^cite_note-107]

Sabalenka entered two indoor hard-court events at the end of 2020. At the [Ostrava Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_J\&T_Banka_Ostrava_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), she came from a set down to defeat qualifiers Coco Gauff and [Sara Sorribes Tormo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sara_Sorribes_Tormo). She defeated [Jennifer Brady](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Brady)[^cite_note-108] and No. 14 Victoria Azarenka in straight sets to win her second title of the season.[^cite_note-109] At the same tournament, she also won the [doubles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_J\&T_Banka_Ostrava_Open_%E2%80%93_Doubles) event with Elise Mertens, defeating [Gabriela Dabrowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriela_Dabrowski) and [Luisa Stefani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luisa_Stefani) in the final. At the [Linz Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Upper_Austria_Ladies_Linz_%E2%80%93_Singles), Sabalenka defeated [Jasmine Paolini](46663470.md) and qualifiers Stefanie Vögele and Océane Dodin to reach the semifinals. She defeated [Barbora Krejčíková](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbora_Krej%C4%8D%C3%ADkov%C3%A1) to reach the final.[^cite_note-110] In the final, she defeated No. 21 Elise Mertens in straight sets to capture her third title of the season.[^cite_note-111] For the first time, she finished the year at the top 10.[^cite_note-112]

### 2021: Major semifinals, singles No. 2, doubles No. 1



Sabalenka at the [2021 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_French_Open)

Sabalenka entered her first tournament in 2021 at the [Abu Dhabi Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Abu_Dhabi_Women's_Tennis_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles). She defeated Polona Hercog, Ajla Tomljanović, and Ons Jabeur,[^cite_note-113] without dropping a set, to reach the quarterfinals. She defeated No. 19 Elena Rybakina in three sets to reach the semifinals. She defeated No. 22 Maria Sakkari[^cite_note-114] and [Veronika Kudermetova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veronika_Kudermetova) in straight sets to win her third straight WTA title, extending her winning streak to 15 matches and catapulting her to a new ranking of No. 7.[^cite_note-115]

At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated [Viktória Hrunčáková](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vikt%C3%B3ria_Hrun%C4%8D%C3%A1kov%C3%A1), Daria Kasatkina, and [Ann Li](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ann_Li_\(tennis\)) to reach the fourth round, where she lost to No. 11 [Serena Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serena_Williams) in three sets. Nonetheless, at the same tournament, Sabalenka won her second major [doubles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_doubles) title with Elise Mertens, defeating Barbora Krejčíková and Kateřina Siniaková in the final.[^cite_note-116] By winning the title, she ascended to world No. 1 in the doubles rankings for the first time in her career.[^cite_note-117]

As the defending champion at the [Qatar Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Qatar_Total_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), Sabalenka lost her opening match to Garbiñe Muguruza.[^cite_note-118] At the [Dubai Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Dubai_Tennis_Championships_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated [Alizé Cornet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aliz%C3%A9_Cornet) and No. 24 Anett Kontaveit to reach the quarterfinals, where she lost again to No. 16 Garbiñe Muguruza in three sets.[^cite_note-119]

At the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka came from a set down to defeat qualifier [Tsvetana Pironkova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsvetana_Pironkova) in her opening match. She defeated Veronika Kudermetova and No. 20 Markéta Vondroušová in straight sets to reach the quarterfinals. In the quarterfinals, she lost to world No. 1 Ashleigh Barty in three sets.[^cite_note-120]

Sabalenka started her clay season at the [Stuttgart Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Porsche_Tennis_Grand_Prix_%E2%80%93_Singles). She defeated Zhang Shuai and qualifier [Anna-Lena Friedsam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna-Lena_Friedsam), without dropping a set, to reach the quarterfinals. She defeated Anett Kontaveit in three sets to reach the semifinals. She upset No. 3 Simona Halep in straight sets to reach the final. In the final, she lost again to world No. 1 Ashleigh Barty in three sets.[^cite_note-121]

At the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated qualifier Vera Zvonareva, Daria Kasatkina, and Jessica Pegula, without dropping a set, to reach the quarterfinals.[^cite_note-122] She reached the semifinals as No. 16 Elise Mertens retired while trailing by a set.[^cite_note-123] She defeated [Anastasia Pavlyuchenkova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anastasia_Pavlyuchenkova) in straight sets to reach her second consecutive final on clay.[^cite_note-124] In the final, she upset world No. 1 Ashleigh Barty in three sets to win her tenth WTA title and first on clay.[^cite_note-125] As a result, her ranking moved to world No. 4.[^cite_note-WTAMadrid2021-126]

At the [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated Sara Sorribes Tormo before losing to Coco Gauff.[^cite_note-127] At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated qualifier [Ana Konjuh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ana_Konjuh) and Aliaksandra Sasnovich before losing to Anastasia Pavlyuchenkova.[^cite_note-128]

Sabalenka started her grass season at the [German Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_WTA_German_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles). She lost her opening match against Madison Keys.[^cite_note-129] Nonetheless, Sabalenka and her compatriot Victoria Azarenka won the [doubles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_WTA_German_Open_%E2%80%93_Doubles) event, defeating the top-seeded pair of Demi Schuurs and [Nicole Melichar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicole_Melichar) in the final.[^cite_note-130] At the [Eastbourne International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Eastbourne_International_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated qualifier [Bernarda Pera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernarda_Pera) and Alison Riske-Amritraj, without dropping a set, to reach the quarterfinals. In the quarterfinals, she lost to qualifier Camila Giorgi in three sets.[^cite_note-131]

At the [Wimbledon Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Women's_Singles), Sabalenka defeated qualifier Monica Niculescu in the first round. In the second round, she came from a set down to defeat wildcard entrant Katie Boulter. In the third round, she defeated qualifier [Camila Osorio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camila_Osorio). She defeated No. 20 Elena Rybakina in three sets to reach her first major quarterfinal.[^cite_note-132] She defeated No. 24 Ons Jabeur in straight sets to reach her first major semifinal, becoming the third Belarusian woman to reach Wimbledon semifinals following [Natasha Zvereva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natasha_Zvereva) in 1998 and Victoria Azarenka in 2011 and 2012.[^cite_note-133] In the semifinals, she lost to No. 13 [Karolína Plíšková](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karol%C3%ADna_Pl%C3%AD%C5%A1kov%C3%A1) in three sets.[^cite_note-134] As a result, she reached a career-high ranking of No. 3.[^cite_note-135]

Sabalenka started her North American hard court season at the [Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_National_Bank_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles). She defeated wildcard entrants [Sloane Stephens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sloane_Stephens) and [Rebecca Marino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebecca_Marino) to reach the quarterfinals. She defeated No. 15 Victoria Azarenka to reach the semifinals,[^cite_note-136] where she lost again to No. 6 Karolína Plíšková in straight sets.[^cite_note-137] At the [Cincinnati Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Western_&_Southern_Open_-_Women's_Singles), she lost her opening-round match against [Paula Badosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paula_Badosa).[^cite_note-138] Despite the loss, she reached a career-high ranking of No. 2.[^cite_note-139]

At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated [Nina Stojanović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nina_Stojanovi%C4%87) in the first round in three sets. She defeated [Tamara Zidanšek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamara_Zidan%C5%A1ek), No. 29 Danielle Collins, No. 16 Elise Mertens,[^cite_note-140] and No. 9 Barbora Krejčíková, without dropping a set, to reach her second major semifinal.[^cite_note-141] In the semifinals, she lost to [Leylah Fernandez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leylah_Fernandez) in three sets.[^cite_note-142]

Due to a positive COVID-19 test, Sabalenka was not able to play at [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_BNP_Paribas_Open_-_Women's_Singles).[^cite_note-143] Sabalenka qualified for the [WTA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_WTA_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles) in singles for the first time.[^cite_note-144] She defeated [Iga Świątek](51838558.md),[^cite_note-145] but was eliminated by Paula Badosa[^cite_note-146] and Maria Sakkari[^cite_note-147] in the round-robin stage.

### 2022: US Open semifinal

Sabalenka struggled with her serve at the beginning of 2022. At the [Adelaide International 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Adelaide_International_1_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she lost her opening match against [Kaja Juvan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaja_Juvan);[^cite_note-148] at the [Adelaide International 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Adelaide_International_2_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she lost her first round to qualifier [Rebecca Peterson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebecca_Peterson).[^cite_note-149] At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she managed to dig out three-set wins against wildcard entrant [Storm Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Storm_Sanders),[^cite_note-Kane2022-150] [Wang Xinyu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Xinyu),[^cite_note-151] and Markéta Vondroušová,[^cite_note-152] but lost in the fourth round to [Kaia Kanepi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaia_Kanepi) in a three-set match, which ended in a third-set super-tiebreak.[^cite_note-153][^cite_note-154]

At the [Dubai Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Dubai_Tennis_Championships_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated qualifier [Marta Kostyuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marta_Kostyuk) before losing to Petra Kvitová.[^cite_note-155] At the [Qatar Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Qatar_Total_Open_-_Singles), Sabalenka beat wildcard entrant Alizé Cornet[^cite_note-156] and [Jil Teichmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jil_Teichmann), without dropping a set, to reach the quarterfinals.[^cite_note-157] In the quarterfinals, she lost to No. 8 and eventual champion Iga Świątek in straight sets.[^cite_note-158]

At the [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka lost her opening match against Jasmine Paolini.[^cite_note-159] In [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she lost her opening match against [Irina-Camelia Begu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irina-Camelia_Begu).[^cite_note-160]

At the [Charleston Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Credit_One_Charleston_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), Sabalenka lost in the third round to Amanda Anisimova.[^cite_note-161] At the [Stuttgart Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Porsche_Tennis_Grand_Prix_-_Singles), Sabalenka defeated [Bianca Andreescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bianca_Andreescu)[^cite_note-162] and No. 6 Anett Kontaveit in three sets to reach the semifinals. She defeated No. 3 Paula Badosa in straight sets to reach her second straight Stuttgart final.[^cite_note-163] In the final, she lost again to world No. 1 Iga Świątek in straight sets.[^cite_note-164]

As the defending champion at the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka was knocked out by Amanda Anisimova again in the first round.[^cite_note-165] At the [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated Zhang Shuai and No. 11 Jessica Pegula, without dropping a set, to reach the quarterfinals. She defeated Amanda Anisimova in three sets to reach the semifinals. In the semifinals, she lost to world No. 1 Iga Świątek for the third time in 2022.[^cite_note-166] At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated [Chloé Paquet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chlo%C3%A9_Paquet) and Madison Brengle before losing to Camila Giorgi.[^cite_note-167]

Sabalenka began the grass-court season at the [Libéma Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Lib%C3%A9ma_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles). She defeated [Kateryna Baindl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kateryna_Baindl) and wildcard entrant [Arianne Hartono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arianne_Hartono), without dropping a set, to reach the quarterfinals. She defeated Alison Van Uytvanck in three sets to reach the semifinals. She defeated [Shelby Rogers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shelby_Rogers) in straight sets to make the final. In the final, she lost to Ekaterina Alexandrova in straight sets.[^cite_note-168] Due to Wimbledon's [ruling on Russian and Belarusian players](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wimbledon_ban_on_Russian_and_Belarusian_players), Sabalenka was banned from participating in the event, cutting her grass season short.[^cite_note-169]

Sabalenka opened the US hardcourt swing in [Silicon Valley Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Silicon_Valley_Classic_%E2%80%93_Singles). She came from a set down to defeat lucky loser [Caroline Dolehide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caroline_Dolehide) in her opening match. In the quarterfinals, she lost to No. 12 and eventual champion Daria Kasatkina in three sets. At the [Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_National_Bank_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated Sara Sorribes Tormo before losing to No. 11 Coco Gauff.[^cite_note-170] At the [Cincinnati Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Western_&_Southern_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka reached the last 16 as qualifier Anna Kalinskaya retired while trailing by a set.[^cite_note-171] She defeated wildcard entrant Shelby Rogers in three sets to reach the quarterfinals. She defeated Zhang Shuai in straight sets to reach the semifinals.[^cite_note-172] In the semifinals, she lost to qualifier and eventual champion [Caroline Garcia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caroline_Garcia) in three sets.[^cite_note-173]

At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated qualifier [Catherine Harrison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catherine_Harrison_\(tennis\)) in the first round.[^cite_note-174] In the second round, she came from a set down to defeat Kaia Kanepi.[^cite_note-175] In the third round, she defeated qualifier [Clara Burel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clara_Burel) in straight sets. In the fourth round, she came from a set down to defeat No. 19 Danielle Collins.[^cite_note-176] She defeated No. 22 Karolína Plíšková in straight sets to reach her second consecutive US Open semifinal.[^cite_note-177] In the semifinals, she was defeated by world No. 1 Iga Świątek for the fourth time this season.[^cite_note-178]

At the [San Diego Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_San_Diego_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated wildcard entrant Sloane Stephens before losing to qualifier Donna Vekić.[^cite_note-179] At the [Guadalajara Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Guadalajara_Open_Akron_%E2%80%93_Singles), she lost her opening match against [Liudmila Samsonova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liudmila_Samsonova).[^cite_note-180]

Sabalenka qualified for the [WTA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_WTA_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles) for a second straight year.[^cite_note-181][^cite_note-182] She defeated world No. 2 Ons Jabeur[^cite_note-183] and No. 3 Jessica Pegula[^cite_note-184] in the round-robin stage, and No. 1 Iga Świątek in the semifinals.[^cite_note-185][^cite_note-186] She became the fourth woman to defeat the top 3 players at the same tournament, joining [Steffi Graf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steffi_Graf) (1999 French Open), Serena Williams (2002 Miami Open), and [Venus Williams](167378.md) (2008 WTA Finals). She lost her group match against Maria Sakkari[^cite_note-187] and lost in the final to Caroline Garcia in straight sets.[^cite_note-188]

### 2023: Australian Open champion, No. 1

See also: [2023 Aryna Sabalenka tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Aryna_Sabalenka_tennis_season)



Sabalenka at the [2023 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_US_Open_\(tennis\))

Sabalenka began her 2023 season at the [Adelaide International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Adelaide_International_1_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles). She defeated Liudmila Samsonova,[^cite_note-189] Markéta Vondroušová, and Irina-Camelia Begu[^cite_note-190] to reach the final. She defeated qualifier [Linda Nosková](67929086.md) to win the title, without dropping a set en route.[^cite_note-191]

Seeded fifth at the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated [Tereza Martincová](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tereza_Martincov%C3%A1), Shelby Rogers, Elise Mertens,[^cite_note-192] and No. 10 Belinda Bencic[^cite_note-193] to reach her first-ever Australian Open quarterfinal. She then beat Donna Vekić to reach her fourth Grand Slam semifinal.[^cite_note-194] She beat [Magda Linette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magda_Linette) to secure her 10th straight-set victory of 2023 and make her first ever major final.[^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196] In the final, she defeated No. 25 [Elena Rybakina](55541443.md) in three sets to win her first major title.[^cite_note-\:0-197][^cite_note-198] She became the second Belarusian to ever win a major singles title after Victoria Azarenka in 2012 and 2013.[^cite_note-199][^cite_note-200][^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202] After winning the title in Melbourne, Sabalenka rose back to No. 2 in the WTA rankings.

In [Dubai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Dubai_Tennis_Championships_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated lucky loser [Lauren Davis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lauren_Davis) in her opening match.[^cite_note-203] In the third round, she came from a set down to defeat Jeļena Ostapenko. In the quarterfinals, she fell to the eventual champion Barbora Krejčíková in three sets.[^cite_note-204]

At the [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated [Evgeniya Rodina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evgeniya_Rodina) in her opening match.[^cite_note-205] She reached the fourth round as qualifier Lesia Tsurenko withdrew.[^cite_note-206] She defeated Barbora Krejčíková in three sets to reach the quarterfinals.[^cite_note-207] She defeated sixth seed Coco Gauff[^cite_note-208] and seventh seed Maria Sakkari in straight sets to reach the final.[^cite_note-209] In a rematch of their Australian Open final, she lost to tenth seed Elena Rybakina in straight sets.[^cite_note-\:3-210]

In [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated Shelby Rogers,[^cite_note-211] [Marie Bouzková](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marie_Bouzkov%C3%A1), and Barbora Krejčíková,[^cite_note-212] without dropping a set, to reach the quarterfinals. In the quarterfinals, she was defeated by [Sorana Cîrstea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sorana_C%C3%AErstea) in straight sets.[^cite_note-213]

Sabalenka started her clay court season at the [Stuttgart Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Porsche_Tennis_Grand_Prix_%E2%80%93_Singles). She defeated Barbora Krejčíková in straight sets to reach the quarterfinals.[^cite_note-214] She defeated wildcard entrant Paula Badosa in three sets to reach the semifinals.[^cite_note-215] She defeated [Anastasia Potapova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anastasia_Potapova) in straight sets to make her third straight Stuttgart final.[^cite_note-216] In a rematch of [last year's final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Porsche_Tennis_Grand_Prix_%E2%80%93_Singles), she was defeated by world No. 1 Iga Świątek in straight sets.[^cite_note-217]

In [Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated Sorana Cîrstea[^cite_note-218] and wildcard entrants Camila Osorio and [Mirra Andreeva](71915009.md),[^cite_note-219] without dropping a set, to reach the quarterfinals. In the quarterfinals, she came from a set down to beat [Mayar Sherif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayar_Sherif).[^cite_note-220] She defeated ninth seed Maria Sakkari in straight sets to reach her second Madrid final.[^cite_note-221] In the final, she defeated world No. 1 Iga Świątek in three sets to win her second Madrid title and fifth WTA 1000 title.[^cite_note-222]

In [Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka suffered her first early defeat of the year, losing her opening match to Sofia Kenin.[^cite_note-223] At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated Marta Kostyuk,[^cite_note-224] qualifier [Iryna Shymanovich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iryna_Shymanovich),[^cite_note-225] [Kamilla Rakhimova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamilla_Rakhimova),[^cite_note-226] and Sloane Stephens,[^cite_note-227] without dropping a set, to reach her first French Open quarterfinal. She defeated Elina Svitolina in straight sets to reach her first French Open semifinal.[^cite_note-228] In the semifinals, she lost to [Karolína Muchová](55266410.md) in three sets, despite leading 5–2 and holding match point.[^cite_note-229]

Sabalenka began her grass court season at the [German Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_WTA_German_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles). She defeated qualifier Vera Zvonareva before losing to wildcard entrant Veronika Kudermetova. At the [Wimbledon Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated [Panna Udvardy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panna_Udvardy) in the first round.[^cite_note-230] In the second round, she came from a set down to defeat [Varvara Gracheva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varvara_Gracheva).[^cite_note-231] She defeated Anna Blinkova,[^cite_note-232] Ekaterina Alexandrova,[^cite_note-233] and Madison Keys,[^cite_note-234] without dropping a set, to reach her fourth consecutive major semifinal. In the semifinals, she lost to sixth seed Ons Jabeur in three sets.[^cite_note-235]

Sabalenka started her North American hard court season in [Montréal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_National_Bank_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles). She defeated Petra Martić[^cite_note-236] before losing to Liudmila Samsonova.[^cite_note-237] In [Cincinnati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Western_&_Southern_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated qualifier Ann Li in three sets in her opening match. She defeated Daria Kasatkina in straight sets to reach the quarterfinals.[^cite_note-238] In a rematch of their Wimbledon semifinal, she defeated fifth seed Ons Jabeur in straight sets.[^cite_note-239] In a rematch of their French Open semifinal, she fell again to Karolína Muchová in three sets.[^cite_note-240]

At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated [Maryna Zanevska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maryna_Zanevska),[^cite_note-241] [Jodie Burrage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jodie_Burrage),[^cite_note-242] Clara Burel,[^cite_note-243] Daria Kasatkina,[^cite_note-244] and [Zheng Qinwen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zheng_Qinwen),[^cite_note-semis-245] without dropping a set, to reach her third consecutive US Open semifinal. She became the first player to reach the semifinals of all four major events in one year since Serena Williams in 2016.[^cite_note-semis-245] She outlasted Madison Keys after losing the first set 6–0 to reach her first US Open final.[^cite_note-246] In the final, she lost to sixth seed Coco Gauff in three sets.[^cite_note-247] Due to the loss of No. 1 Iga Świątek in the fourth round, Sabalenka became, for the first time, the [world No. 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_WTA_number_1_ranked_singles_tennis_players) tennis player.[^cite_note-248] She became the 29th player to be ranked No. 1 in the world on the WTA Tour, and the second Belarusian, after Victoria Azarenka. She was also the eighth female player to have been ranked No. 1 in the Open Era in both singles and doubles at some point in their careers.[^cite_note-249][^cite_note-250]

After the US Open, Sabalenka played at the [China Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_China_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles). She defeated wildcard entrant Sofia Kenin,[^cite_note-251] qualifier Katie Boulter,[^cite_note-252] and Jasmine Paolini,[^cite_note-253] without dropping a set, to reach the quarterfinals. In the quarterfinals, she lost to fifth seed Elena Rybakina in straight sets.[^cite_note-254]

Sabalenka made her third consecutive appearance at the [WTA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_WTA_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles).[^cite_note-255] She defeated Maria Sakkari[^cite_note-256] and Elena Rybakina[^cite_note-257] but lost to Jessica Pegula[^cite_note-258] in the round-robin stage. In the semifinals, she lost to Iga Świątek in straight sets, who would go on to win the event.[^cite_note-259] As a result, Świątek reclaimed the world No. 1 ranking and Sabalenka finished as year-end No. 2.[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261]

### 2024: Two major titles, year-end No. 1

See also: [2024 Aryna Sabalenka tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Aryna_Sabalenka_tennis_season)



Sabalenka at the [2024 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_US_Open_\(tennis\))

Sabalenka started her 2024 tennis season at the [Brisbane International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Brisbane_International_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles). She defeated [Lucia Bronzetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucia_Bronzetti),[^cite_note-262] [Zhu Lin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhu_Lin_\(tennis\)),[^cite_note-263] Daria Kasatkina,[^cite_note-264] and Victoria Azarenka[^cite_note-265] without dropping a set, to reach the final. In the final, she lost to second seed Elena Rybakina in straight sets.[^cite_note-266]

At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka overcame qualifier [Ella Seidel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ella_Seidel),[^cite_note-267] qualifier [Brenda Fruhvirtová](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brenda_Fruhvirtov%C3%A1),[^cite_note-268] Lesia Tsurenko,[^cite_note-269] Amanda Anisimova,[^cite_note-270] ninth seed Barbora Krejčíková,[^cite_note-271] without dropping a set, to reach the semifinals. In a rematch of their [US Open final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated fourth seed Coco Gauff in straight sets to reach her second consecutive Australian Open final.[^cite_note-272] In the final, she defeated Zheng Qinwen in straight sets,[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274] becoming the first woman to successfully defend her title at the Australian Open since Victoria Azarenka in 2013.[^cite_note-275][^cite_note-276]

At the [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated [Peyton Stearns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peyton_Stearns)[^cite_note-277] and wildcard entrant [Emma Raducanu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emma_Raducanu)[^cite_note-278] before losing to [Emma Navarro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emma_Navarro).[^cite_note-279] In [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated Paula Badosa[^cite_note-280] before losing to [Anhelina Kalinina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anhelina_Kalinina).[^cite_note-281]

Sabalenka started her clay season in [Stuttgart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Porsche_Tennis_Grand_Prix_%E2%80%93_Singles). She reached the quarterfinals when Paula Badosa retired,[^cite_note-282] but was eliminated by sixth seed Markéta Vondroušová in three sets.[^cite_note-283] As the defending champion in [Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka reached the quarterfinals after three-set wins over Magda Linette,[^cite_note-284] wildcard entrant [Robin Montgomery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robin_Montgomery),[^cite_note-285] and Danielle Collins.[^cite_note-286] She defeated Mirra Andreeva in straight sets to reach the semifinals.[^cite_note-287] In the semifinals, she edged an epic three-set victory over fourth seed Elena Rybakina.[^cite_note-288] In a rematch of the [previous year's final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she faced world No. 1 Iga Świątek. Sabalenka had three championship points during the match, but eventually lost.[^cite_note-289]

In [Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka came from a set down to defeat qualifier [Katie Volynets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katie_Volynets) in her opening match. In the third round, she defeated Dayana Yastremska in straight sets. In the fourth round, she came from a set down to defeat Elina Svitolina.[^cite_note-290] She defeated ninth seed Jeļena Ostapenko[^cite_note-Madrid2024QF-291] and Danielle Collins in straight sets to reach the final.[^cite_note-Madrid2024QF-291] In the final, she faced again Iga Świątek, and lost in straight sets.[^cite_note-292]

At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated [Erika Andreeva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erika_Andreeva),[^cite_note-293] qualifier [Moyuka Uchijima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moyuka_Uchijima),[^cite_note-294] Paula Badosa[^cite_note-295] and Emma Navarro,[^cite_note-296] without dropping a set, to reach the quarterfinals. In the quarterfinals, she was hampered by stomach issues[^cite_note-297] and lost to Mirra Andreeva in three sets.[^cite_note-298] Due to her loss, her ranking dropped to No. 3.[^cite_note-299]

Sabalenka suffered a right shoulder injury that forced her to retire during her quarterfinal match against Anna Kalinskaya at the [Berlin Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Berlin_Ladies_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles) in June[^cite_note-300] and she withdrew from the [Wimbledon Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), having failed to recover in time to play.[^cite_note-301]



Sabalenka lifting the 2024 US Open singles trophy

Sabalenka returned from injury at the [Washington DC Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Mubadala_Citi_DC_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles). She defeated qualifier Kamilla Rakhimova[^cite_note-302] and Victoria Azarenka[^cite_note-303] to reach the semifinals, where she lost to Marie Bouzková in three sets.[^cite_note-304] At the [Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_National_Bank_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated [Yuan Yue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuan_Yue) and Katie Boulter to reach the quarterfinals,[^cite_note-305] where she lost to Amanda Anisimova in straight sets.[^cite_note-306]

At the [Cincinnati Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Cincinnati_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated [Elisabetta Cocciaretto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elisabetta_Cocciaretto),[^cite_note-307] Elina Svitolina,[^cite_note-308] and Liudmila Samsonova[^cite_note-309] to reach the semifinals. She defeated world No. 1 Iga Świątek in the semifinals,[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-\:5-311] and defeated sixth seed Jessica Pegula to win the title, without dropping a set en route.[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-\:6-313] Her ranking moved back to No. 2.[^cite_note-\:5-311][^cite_note-\:6-313]

At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated qualifier [Priscilla Hon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Priscilla_Hon)[^cite_note-314] and Lucia Bronzetti in straight sets.[^cite_note-315] In the third round, she came from a set down to defeat Ekaterina Alexandrova.[^cite_note-316] She defeated Elise Mertens,[^cite_note-317] seventh seed Zheng Qinwen[^cite_note-318] and Emma Navarro,[^cite_note-319] without dropping a set, to reach her second straight US Open final. She defeated sixth seed Jessica Pegula in straight sets to win her first US Open singles title and third major singles title.[^cite_note-320][^cite_note-321] She became the fifth woman to win the Australian Open and the US Open in the same year since 1988.[^cite_note-322]

Sabalenka continued her great form in China. At the [China Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_China_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated qualifier [Mananchaya Sawangkaew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mananchaya_Sawangkaew),[^cite_note-323] [Ashlyn Krueger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashlyn_Krueger)[^cite_note-324] and Madison Keys,[^cite_note-325] without dropping a set, to reach the quarterfinals. In the quarterfinals, she lost to Karolína Muchová in three sets.[^cite_note-326] At the [Wuhan Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Wuhan_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), where she won back-to-back in 2018 and 2019, before the tournament was suspended due to COVID-19 from 2020 until 2023, Sabalenka defeated Kateřina Siniaková in straight sets in her opening match.[^cite_note-327] In the third round, she came back from a set down against Yulia Putintseva.[^cite_note-328] In the quarterfinals, she defeated [Magdalena Fręch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magdalena_Fr%C4%99ch).[^cite_note-329] In the semifinals, she mounted a comeback from a set and a break down against fourth seed Coco Gauff.[^cite_note-330] In the final, she defeated fifth seed Zheng Qinwen in three sets to lift her third Wuhan Open title and her second WTA 1000 title for the 2024 season.[^cite_note-331]

After 11 months off the top in October, Sabalenka regained the WTA world number-one ranking.[^cite_note-332] Following wins against Zheng Qinwen[^cite_note-333] and Jasmine Paolini in her first two group matches at the [WTA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_WTA_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles),[^cite_note-334] combined with Świątek's loss to Coco Gauff, Sabalenka for the first time would finish as [year-end No. 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_WTA_number_1_ranked_singles_tennis_players).[^cite_note-335][^cite_note-336] She lost her last group match to Elena Rybakina[^cite_note-337] and was defeated by Coco Gauff in the semifinals.[^cite_note-338]

In December, Sabalenka was named [WTA Player of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTA_Awards#Player_of_the_Year).[^cite_note-339][^cite_note-340]

### 2025: US Open title, year-end No. 1

See also: [2025 Aryna Sabalenka tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Aryna_Sabalenka_tennis_season)

Sabalenka started 2025 at the [Brisbane International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Brisbane_International_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles). She defeated [Renata Zarazúa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renata_Zaraz%C3%BAa),[^cite_note-341] Yulia Putintseva,[^cite_note-342] Marie Bouzková,[^cite_note-343] and No. 16 Mirra Andreeva,[^cite_note-344] without dropping a set, to reach the final. In the final, she came back from a set down against qualifier [Polina Kudermetova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polina_Kudermetova) to win the title.[^cite_note-345][^cite_note-346] As the two-time defending champion at the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated Sloane Stephens,[^cite_note-347] [Jéssica Bouzas Maneiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%A9ssica_Bouzas_Maneiro),[^cite_note-348] [Clara Tauson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clara_Tauson),[^cite_note-349] and No. 15 Mirra Andreeva, without dropping a set, to reach the quarterfinals. She defeated Anastasia Pavlyuchenkova in three sets to reach the semifinals. She defeated No.12 Paula Badosa in straight sets to advance to her third straight Australian Open final.[^cite_note-350] In the final, she lost to No. 14 Madison Keys in three sets.[^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352]

At the [Qatar Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Qatar_TotalEnergies_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), Sabalenka lost her opening match against Ekaterina Alexandrova.[^cite_note-353] At the [Dubai Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Dubai_Tennis_Championships_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she defeated qualifier Veronika Kudermetov[^cite_note-wtatennis.com-354] before losing to Clara Tauson.[^cite_note-355]



&#x20;Sabalenka at a 2025 Miami Open press conference

At the [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated [McCartney Kessler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McCartney_Kessler),[^cite_note-356][^cite_note-tennis.com-357] Lucia Bronzetti,[^cite_note-358] lucky loser [Sonay Kartal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonay_Kartal),[^cite_note-359] and Liudmila Samsonova,[^cite_note-360] without dropping a set, to reach the semifinals. In a rematch of their Australian Open final, she defeated fifth seed Madison Keys in straight sets to reach her second Indian Wells final.[^cite_note-361] In the final, she lost to ninth seed Mirra Andreeva in three sets.[^cite_note-362]

At the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated [Viktoriya Tomova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viktoriya_Tomova) in her opening match.[^cite_note-363] She reached the fourth round as qualifier [Elena-Gabriela Ruse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elena-Gabriela_Ruse) retired while trailing by a set.[^cite_note-364] She defeated defending champion Danielle Collins,[^cite_note-365] ninth seed Zheng Qinwen,[^cite_note-366][^cite_note-367] sixth seed Jasmine Paolini,[^cite_note-368] and fourth seed Jessica Pegula to win the title, without dropping a set en route.[^cite_note-369][^cite_note-370] She became the eighth player to have surpassed the 10,000 points mark in the WTA rankings.[^cite_note-371]

Starting her clay season at the [Stuttgart Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Porsche_Tennis_Grand_Prix_%E2%80%93_Singles), Sabalenka reached the quarterfinals via walkover when Anastasia Potapova withdrew due to injury.[^cite_note-express.co.uk-372] She defeated Elise Mertens[^cite_note-express.co.uk-372] and fifth seed Jasmine Paolini,[^cite_note-373] without dropping a set, to reach her fourth Stuttgart final. She lost the championship match against Jeļena Ostapenko in straight sets.[^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375]

At the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated qualifier Anna Blinkova in her opening match.[^cite_note-376] In the third round, she came from a set down to defeat Elise Mertens.[^cite_note-377] She defeated Peyton Stearns,[^cite_note-378] Marta Kostyuk,[^cite_note-379] and Elina Svitolina,[^cite_note-380] without dropping a set, to reach her fourth Madrid final. In the final, she defeated fourth seed Coco Gauff in straight sets to claim her third Madrid Open title.[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382] She became the third woman to surpass 11,000 points in the WTA rankings after Serena Williams and Iga Świątek.[^cite_note-383][^cite_note-384]

In [Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated Anastasia Potapova in her opening match.[^cite_note-385] In the third round, she came from a set down to defeat Sofia Kenin.[^cite_note-386] She defeated Marta Kostyuk in straight sets to reach the quarterfinals,[^cite_note-387] where she lost to eighth seed Zheng Qinwen in straight sets.[^cite_note-388][^cite_note-389] At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated Kamilla Rakhimova,[^cite_note-390] Jil Teichmann,[^cite_note-391] [Olga Danilović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olga_Danilovi%C4%87),[^cite_note-392] Amanda Anisimova,[^cite_note-393] and Zheng Qinwen,[^cite_note-394] without dropping a set, to reach the semifinals. In the semifinals, she defeated three-time defending champion Iga Świątek in three sets to reach her first French Open final.[^cite_note-WTARG-395][^cite_note-396] In a rematch of their Madrid Open final, she lost to second seed Coco Gauff in three sets.[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-\:1-398]

Sabalenka started her grass season at the [Berlin Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Berlin_Tennis_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles). She defeated qualifier [Rebeka Masarova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebeka_Masarova) in straight sets to reach the quarterfinals.[^cite_note-399] She defeated No. 11 Elena Rybakina in three sets to reach the semifinals.[^cite_note-400] In the semifinals, she lost to eventual champion Markéta Vondroušová in straight sets.[^cite_note-401] At the [Wimbledon Championships](80007712.md), Sabalenka beat qualifier [Carson Branstine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carson_Branstine),[^cite_note-402] Marie Bouzková,[^cite_note-403] Emma Raducanu,[^cite_note-404] and Elise Mertens,[^cite_note-405] without dropping a set, to reach the quarterfinals. She defeated [Laura Siegemund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Siegemund) in three sets to advance to the semifinals.[^cite_note-406] In the semifinals, she lost to No. 12 Amanda Anisimova in three sets.[^cite_note-407] Despite this loss, she became the first woman to surpass 12,000 points on the WTA rankings since Serena Williams in 2015.[^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409]

Sabalenka started her US hard court season at the [Cincinnati Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Cincinnati_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles). She defeated Markéta Vondroušová in straight sets in her opening match.[^cite_note-410] In the third round, she defeated Emma Raducanu in three sets.[^cite_note-411] She defeated Jessica Bouzas Maneiro in straight sets to advance to the quarterfinals.[^cite_note-412] In the quarterfinals, she lost to ninth seed Elena Rybakina in straight sets.[^cite_note-\:2-413] At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated Rebeka Masarova, Polina Kudermetova, Leylah Fernandez, and [Cristina Bucșa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cristina_Buc%C8%99a),[^cite_note-414] without dropping a set, to reach the quarterfinals. She advanced to the semifinals via walkover when Markéta Vondroušová withdrew due to injury.[^cite_note-415] In a rematch of their [2024 US Open final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she came from a set down to defeat fourth seed Jessica Pegula and reach her third consecutive US Open final.[^cite_note-416][^cite_note-417] In a rematch of their Wimbledon semifinal, she defeated eighth seed Amanda Anisimova in straight sets to win her second US Open title and fourth major title. She became the first player to defend a US Open singles title since Serena Williams in 2014.[^cite_note-CBSUSOpen2025-418][^cite_note-419]

As the three-time defending champion at the [Wuhan Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Wuhan_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), Sabalenka came from a set down to overcome [Rebecca Sramkova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebecca_Sramkova) in her opening match.[^cite_note-420] She defeated Liudmila Samsonova[^cite_note-421] and eight seed Elena Rybakina in straight sets to advance to the semifinals.[^cite_note-422] In the semifinals, she lost to sixth seed Jessica Pegula in three sets.[^cite_note-423]

Sabalenka made her fifth consecutive appearance at the [WTA Finals](81306023.md).[^cite_note-424] She won all her three matches in the round-robin stage against Jasmine Paolini,[^cite_note-425] Jessica Pegula,[^cite_note-426] and Coco Gauff[^cite_note-427] to reach the semifinals. She defeated Amanda Anisimova in three sets to reach her second WTA Finals final.[^cite_note-OlympicsWTA2025-428] In the final, she lost to Elena Rybakina in straight sets.[^cite_note-429]

For the second year in a row, Sabalenka finished as the year-end No. 1.[^cite_note-\:7-430] She also held the No. 1 position uninterrupted through the entire season.[^cite_note-\:7-430] She was also named [WTA Player of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTA_Awards#Player_of_the_Year) for the second year in a row.[^cite_note-431]

On 28 December 2025, Sabalenka (women's singles world No. 1) and [Nick Kyrgios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Kyrgios) (men's singles world No. 671) contested an exhibition match promoted as the *[Battle of the Sexes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_the_Sexes_\(tennis\))* at the [Coca-Cola Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coca-Cola_Arena) in [Dubai](211583.md).[^cite_note-432][^cite_note-433] The event was organized by their shared management agency, Evolve. The match was played as a three-set contest under modified rules: the dimensions of Sabalenka's side of the court were reduced by 9%, and both players were restricted to a single serve per point. In the event of a tie, a 10-point match tiebreak would have been played.[^cite_note-SkySports_Dec2025-434] According to Evolve, the court-size adjustment was intended to "reflect average movement-speed differences between men and women".[^cite_note-435] Kyrgios defeated Sabalenka in straight sets, 6–3, 6–3.[^cite_note-SkySports_Dec2025-434]

### 2026: Sunshine Double, Australian Open and US Open finalist

See also: [2026 Aryna Sabalenka tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Aryna_Sabalenka_tennis_season)

Sabalenka began her 2026 season at the [Brisbane International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Brisbane_International_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles). She defeated Cristina Bucșa,[^cite_note-436] Sorana Cîrstea,[^cite_note-437] fifth seed Madison Keys,[^cite_note-438] Karolína Muchová,[^cite_note-439] and Marta Kostyuk to successfully defend her title, without dropping a set en route.[^cite_note-440]
