At the [Australian Open](81715708.md), Sabalenka defeated wildcard entrant [Tiantsoa Sarah Rakotomanga Rajaonah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiantsoa_Sarah_Rakotomanga_Rajaonah),[^cite_note-441] qualifier [Bai Zhuoxuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bai_Zhuoxuan),[^cite_note-442] Anastasia Potapova,[^cite_note-443] [Victoria Mboko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Mboko),[^cite_note-444] [Iva Jovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iva_Jovic),[^cite_note-445] and Elina Svitolina to reach her fourth consecutive Australian Open final, without dropping a set en route.[^cite_note-446] In a rematch of their [2025 WTA Finals](81306023.md) and [2023 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) finals, she was defeated by fifth seed Elena Rybakina in three sets.[^cite_note-447]

Sabalenka skipped the Middle East swing for fatigue-related reasons.[^cite_note-448] She returned to play at the [Indian Wells Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles). She defeated qualifier [Himeno Sakatsume](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Himeno_Sakatsume),[^cite_note-449] Jaqueline Cristian,[^cite_note-450] Naomi Osaka,[^cite_note-451] tenth seed Victoria Mboko,[^cite_note-WTAIW2026-452] and Linda Nosková[^cite_note-453] to reach her third Indian Wells final, without dropping a set en route. In a rematch of their Australian Open and [2023 Indian Wells Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) finals, she defeated third seed Elena Rybakina to win her first Indian Wells title and tenth WTA 1000 title, saving a match point in the third-set tiebreaker.[^cite_note-454]

At the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka defeated Ann Li,[^cite_note-455] [Caty McNally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caty_McNally),[^cite_note-456] Zheng Qinwen,[^cite_note-457] and [Hailey Baptiste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hailey_Baptiste)[^cite_note-458] to reach the semifinals, without dropping a set en route. In the semifinals, she defeated third seed Elena Rybakina in straight sets to reach her second straight Miami final.[^cite_note-459] In the final, she defeated fourth seed Coco Gauff in three sets to win her second straight Miami Open title.[^cite_note-460] She became the first player since Iga Świątek in 2022 to complete the [Sunshine Double](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Open_\(tennis\)#Sunshine_Double) and the fifth woman overall to complete the Sunshine Double in singles.[^cite_note-WTAMiami2026-461] She also became the first player, male or female, to complete the achievement in both singles and doubles, having previously completed the Sunshine Double in doubles at the [2019 Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_doubles).[^cite_note-Local10Miami-462]

Due to an injury, Sabalenka withdrew from [Stuttgart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Porsche_Tennis_Grand_Prix_%E2%80%93_Singles), where she was runner-up the previous year. She started her clay season with a first round bye in [Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles). She then defeated [Peyton Stearns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peyton_Stearns),[^cite_note-463] and 29th seed [Jaqueline Cristian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaqueline_Cristian) to reach fourth round.[^cite_note-464] In the fourth round, she came back from a set and a break down to defeat [Naomi Osaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naomi_Osaka) to reach her 17th consecutive quarterfinal on the WTA tour.[^cite_note-465] She lost in the querterfinals to [Hailey Baptiste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hailey_Baptiste) after having six match points.[^cite_note-466] She received bye in the first round in [Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), then she defeated Barbora Krejčíková in straight sets, before losing to 26th seed [Sorana Cirstea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sorana_Cirstea) in the third round after being a break and a set up, thus marking an end to her 17 consecutive quarterfinals streak on WTA Tour.[^cite_note-467][^cite_note-468] At the [French Open](83033049.md), Sabalenka reached the quarterfinals after defeating fellow Grand Slam champion Naomi Osaka in the first women's night session match since 2023.[^cite_note-469] However, she then lost to 25th seed [Diana Shnaider](68209798.md) after a [bagel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bagel_\(tennis\)) in the third set, ending her hopes of securing a slam on a natural surface.[^cite_note-470]

Sabalenka began her grass-court season at Berlin, where she defeated [Ekaterina Alexandrova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ekaterina_Alexandrova), after receiving a first round bye.[^cite_note-471] She overcame a second set deficit to defeat [Nikola Bartůňková](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikola_Bart%C5%AF%C5%88kov%C3%A1) to advance to the semifinals,[^cite_note-472] where she lost to Jessica Pegula with a third set bagel.[^cite_note-473] At [Wimbledon](83354904.md), Sabalenka lost in the fourth round to Osaka. This marked the first time since the 2022 French Open that she lost a major match before the quarterfinals, snapping a streak of 14 major quarterfinal appearances.[^cite_note-474]

At the [Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_National_Bank_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), Sabalenka lost to Alexandrova in the fourth round.[^cite_note-475] She had another fourth-round appearance at the [Cincinnati Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Cincinnati_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), but lost to [Sára Bejlek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A1ra_Bejlek) in straight sets.[^cite_note-476] Sabalenka entered the [US Open](84044135.md) being in contention for the world no. 1 ranking.[^cite_note-477] She ultimately reached the final and lost to Rybakina in three sets, ending her bid for a three-peat. She also lost the no. 1 ranking to Rybakina, ending her reign of 99 consecutive weeks atop the rankings, the sixth-longest streak of all time.[^cite_note-478]

## National representation

### Fed Cup

#### Early appearances

Sabalenka represented Belarus at the Junior Fed Cup in 2014, with the team finishing in sixth place.[^cite_note-479] She then made her senior [Fed Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fed_Cup) debut for [Belarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarus_Fed_Cup_team) in April 2016, losing a [dead rubber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dead_rubber) doubles match against [Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia_Fed_Cup_team). Nonetheless, the Belarusian team led by [Victoria Azarenka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Azarenka) and [Aliaksandra Sasnovich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aliaksandra_Sasnovich) won the tie to qualify for the top-tier World Group the following season for the first time in their history.[^cite_note-fed-cup-debut-17]

#### 2017: Surprise runner-up in World Group debut

The Belarus Fed Cup team made their debut in the World Group and ultimately reached the final, despite being the underdogs in all three ties.[^cite_note-2017-sf-preview-480][^cite_note-2017-final-preview-481] Little was expected from the team because they were without their veteran leader Azarenka, who missed the first two ties on maternity leave and the last because of a custody battle.[^cite_note-482] Without her, Belarus was led by Sabalenka and Sasnovich, neither of whom had ever been ranked above No. 76 by the time of the final.[^cite_note-SabWtaRnk-16][^cite_note-sasnovich-rankings-483] However, they did have the advantage of playing all of their ties at home in Minsk.[^cite_note-2017-sf-preview-480][^cite_note-2017-final-preview-481]

The ties in the quarterfinals against the [Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netherlands_Fed_Cup_team) in February and the semi-finals against [Switzerland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Switzerland_Fed_Cup_team) in April both played out in the same way. While Sabalenka lost her opening matches to their opponents' respective top-ranked players of [Kiki Bertens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiki_Bertens) and [Timea Bacsinszky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timea_Bacsinszky), Sasnovich was able to give Belarus a 2–1 lead in each instance.[^cite_note-484] Sabalenka then clinched both ties, with wins over [Michaëlla Krajicek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Micha%C3%ABlla_Krajicek) and No. 54 [Viktorija Golubic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viktorija_Golubic), respectively.[^cite_note-485][^cite_note-486] She was only ranked No. 125 at the time of the semifinal, with no career tour match wins outside of Fed Cup.[^cite_note-487]

> "I've never felt so much emotion in a match. When you play at home and you are down 0–1 and you have to win and you fight with yourself... I just started crying because it was such an important match."

— Sabalenka on her Fed Cup rubber win over Stephens

[^cite_note-fed-cup-quote-488]

On the opening day of the final against the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Fed_Cup_team), Sabalenka upset the reigning US Open champion and world No. 13, [Sloane Stephens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sloane_Stephens), to level the tie after Sasnovich lost her first rubber to No. 10, [CoCo Vandeweghe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CoCo_Vandeweghe).[^cite_note-489][^cite_note-fed-cup-quote-488] The next day began with Sabalenka losing to Vandeweghe, before Sasnovich again levelled the tie by defeating Stephens. Sabalenka and Sasnovich were then selected for the decisive doubles rubber for the Fed Cup crown, but the duo were comprehensively defeated by Vandeweghe and [Shelby Rogers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shelby_Rogers).[^cite_note-fed-cup-final-27]

Despite finishing as runner-up, Belarus's Fed Cup success helped popularize women's tennis in Belarus, and vaulted Sabalenka and Sasnovich into international prominence. Sasnovich said, "When we played the quarterfinals and semi-finals in Minsk, a lot of people were coming to see our matches. They finally saw tennis in life, and it's like a popularization... I want my country to improve even more in tennis, because I think we can have even more from Belarus."[^cite_note-sasnovich-quote-490]

#### 2018–19: Avoiding demotion, another semifinal

Belarus was unable to repeat their 2017 Fed Cup success in 2018. Their quarterfinal tie was held in Minsk against [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germany_Fed_Cup_team). Although Sabalenka won both of her singles rubbers, Sasnovich and [Vera Lapko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vera_Lapko) lost each of theirs to set up a decisive doubles rubber. Sabalenka and doubles specialist [Lidziya Marozava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lidziya_Marozava) were selected for the match, with Sabalenka playing on short rest directly after her last singles match. After taking the first set against [Anna-Lena Grönefeld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna-Lena_Gr%C3%B6nefeld) and [Tatjana Maria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatjana_Maria), they ultimately lost the rubber and the tie.[^cite_note-491]

Their next tie was again contested in Minsk as part of the [World Group play-offs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Fed_Cup_World_Group_play-offs), with [Slovakia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slovakia_Fed_Cup_team) competing to take Belarus's place in the World Group the following season. Sabalenka and Sasnovich each split their two singles rubbers, with Sabalenka being upset by [Viktória Kužmová](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vikt%C3%B3ria_Ku%C5%BEmov%C3%A1).[^cite_note-492] Doubles specialists Lapko and Marozava were chosen for the final rubber and the pair won the match to keep Belarus in the World Group for 2019.[^cite_note-493]

In the [2019 Fed Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Fed_Cup), Belarus were drawn against Germany in the quarterfinals for the second consecutive year. After Sasnovich won the opening rubber against Maria, Sabalenka won both of her singles rubbers against [Andrea Petkovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_Petkovic) and [Laura Siegemund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Siegemund) to clinch the tie.[^cite_note-494] They advanced to face [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_Fed_Cup_team) in the semi-finals. Only two players from each team participated: Sabalenka and Azarenka for Belarus, and Ashleigh Barty and [Samantha Stosur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samantha_Stosur) for Australia. Both Sabalenka and Azarenka defeated Stosur, but lost to Barty. In the decisive doubles rubber, Barty and Stosur won in three sets to eliminate Belarus.[^cite_note-495]

## Rivalries

### Elena Rybakina

Main article: [Rybakina–Sabalenka rivalry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rybakina%E2%80%93Sabalenka_rivalry)

Sabalenka and [Elena Rybakina](55541443.md) have played each other 18 times since 2019, with Sabalenka leading their head-to-head 10–8 whereas Rybakina leads the finals 5–2.[^cite_note-496] Both players are known for their aggressive baseline play, resulting in fast-paced and intense matches.

They have faced off in three major finals with the [2023 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Australian_Open_-_Women's_singles)[^cite_note-\:0-197] won by Sabalenka, and the [2026 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Australian_Open_-_Women's_singles)[^cite_note-497] and [US Open](84044135.md) both won by Rybakina.

Their other notable encounters include the [2023 BNP Paribas Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) finals,[^cite_note-498] [2024 Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) semifinal,[^cite_note-499] [2025 WTA Finals](81306023.md) finals,[^cite_note-500] the [2026 BNP Paribas Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) final,[^cite_note-501] and the [2026 US Open](80928175.md) final.[^cite_note-502]

### Iga Świątek

Sabalenka and [Iga Świątek](51838558.md) have met 13 times since 2021, with Świątek in control of the head-to-head at 8–5.[^cite_note-iga_h2h-503] Their rivalry has been seen as becoming one of the greatest in women's tennis.[^cite_note-\:52-504][^cite_note-505] Their most praised matches include the [2024 Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) final, where Świątek defeated defending champion Sabalenka in three sets in 3 hours and 11 minutes, saving three championship points in the process, and the [2023 Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) final, where Sabalenka won in three sets in 2 hours and 25 minutes against Świątek to claim her second Madrid title.[^cite_note-\:32-506][^cite_note-\:42-507][^cite_note-508] Sabalenka and Świątek played each other at the [2025 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), which Sabalenka won in three sets.[^cite_note-WTARG-395]

### Coco Gauff

Sabalenka and [Coco Gauff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coco_Gauff) have met 13 times since 2020, with Sabalenka edging the head-to-head at 7–6.[^cite_note-coco_h2h-509] They have played each other multiple times at [majors](197638.md), the first being the [2023 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) final.[^cite_note-510] Sabalenka lost that match despite winning the first set.[^cite_note-511] Sabalenka defeated Gauff at the [2024 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) semifinals on her way to defending the title.[^cite_note-512][^cite_note-513] Sabalenka and Gauff faced off again in the final of the [2025 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_French_Open), with Gauff overtaking Sabalenka for her first French Open Major Championship win and second Grand Slam overall.[^cite_note-\:1-398] Sabalenka would avenge that loss by knocking Gauff out of the [2025 WTA Finals](81306023.md) where Gauff was the defending champion.[^cite_note-514] Sabalenka would meet Gauff for the first time in 2026 in the [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) finals, where Sabalenka battled through Gauff in three sets to win Miami for the second time, as well as the [Sunshine Double](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunshine_Double).[^cite_note-WTAMiami2026-461]

### Jessica Pegula

Sabalenka has developed a fairly storied rivalry with [Jessica Pegula](32944701.md). Their consistency on hard courts leads to them frequently facing each other in the latter stages of events. The two have met 14 times since 2020, with Sabalenka controlling the head-to-head 10–4.[^cite_note-515] Pegula would win their first meeting in the third round of the [2020 Western & Southern Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Western_&_Southern_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) in three sets. They would face off at the [2024 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) final in which Sabalenka would win 7–5, 7–5 to earn her first US Open title.[^cite_note-\:1-398] The two would have a rematch of their US Open final in the [2025 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) semifinals, where Sabalenka would win a close three-set match 4–6, 6–3, 6–4 on her way to defending her US Open title.[^cite_note-\:2-413] Pegula would snap Sabalenka's 20-match winning streak as well as her 2025 tiebreak record in their following match in [Wuhan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Wuhan_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), before pulling out a three-set win 2–6, 6–4, 7–6(2).[^cite_note-\:3-210] They faced each other once more in the Riyadh [2025 WTA Finals](81306023.md), where Sabalenka defeated Pegula in three sets.[^cite_note-\:4-516] The pair's first meeting on grass came at the [2026 Berlin Tennis Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Berlin_Tennis_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), which Pegula won in three sets, bageling Sabalenka in the final set.[^cite_note-517]

### Jasmine Paolini

Sabalenka has developed a recurring rivalry with [Jasmine Paolini](46663470.md), which she leads 6–2. The pair first met at the [2017 Ilkley Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Aegon_Ilkley_Trophy_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), where Paolini defeated Sabalenka in three sets. Paolini later earned another notable win at the [2022 Indian Wells Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Indian_Wells_Open), defeating Sabalenka 2–6, 6–3, 6–3.[^cite_note-518] Sabalenka has otherwise controlled the matchup, winning their later meetings at events including [Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Open_\(tennis\)), the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Open_\(tennis\)), the [Stuttgart Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porsche_Tennis_Grand_Prix), and the [WTA Finals](1356185.md). Their matches have contrasted Sabalenka's power-based baseline game with Paolini's speed, court coverage, and ability to extend rallies.[^cite_note-519]

### Amanda Anisimova

Sabalenka has also developed a rivalry with [Amanda Anisimova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amanda_Anisimova). The pair have played 11 times with Sabalenka trailing 5–6. They've met six times in Grand Slams including 3 consecutive major contests in 2025 where they're tied 3–3. Anisimova defeated Sabalenka en route to her first major semifinal at [2019 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles)[^cite_note-WTAAnisimova-72] and also in the semifinal of [2025 Wimbledon](80007712.md)[^cite_note-520] to reach her first major final. They met in the final of [2025 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) where Sabalenka won 6–3, 7–6<sup>(7–3)</sup> to win her fourth major.[^cite_note-CBSUSOpen2025-418] In 2025, they met again in the semifinal to [WTA Finals](81306023.md) won by Sabalenka.[^cite_note-OlympicsWTA2025-428]

## Playing style



Sabalenka serving at the [2019 Sydney International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Sydney_International_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles)

Sabalenka is an [aggressive baseliner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aggressive_baseliner) whose game is characterised by a powerful [serve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serve_\(tennis\)) and equally powerful [groundstrokes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groundstroke). Her game is based around taking control of points, with a knack for turning defensive positions into offensive opportunities. Due to her aggression, she usually hits a high number of both winners and unforced errors off her groundstrokes, which are hit with relentless pace and depth.[^cite_note-style-against-pliskova2-521] At the [2024 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), she struck her forehand at an average speed of 129 km/h (80 mph).[^cite_note-TennisBiomech-522] Sabalenka's big serve is central to her game: in 2024 she won 62.7% of points on her serve, ranking fifth among the players on tour, and won 78.5% of her service games.[^cite_note-523]

Sabalenka's improved consistency on serve was the key which allowed her to ascend to the top of the WTA rankings. In the past, she struggled with an inconsistent serve, leading to a high double fault count; she served 166 double faults in 2020, the most of any player.[^cite_note-524] She notably suffered from the [yips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yips) from the [2021 WTA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_WTA_Finals) and into 2022, serving 152 double faults in 11 matches, an average of 14 double faults a match.[^cite_note-Kane2022-150] Following the [2022 Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Canadian_Open_\(tennis\)), she hired biomechanics expert Gavin Macmillan to revamp her serve.[^cite_note-525] Sabalenka served a total of 440 double faults in 2022, but her serve quickly saw improvements in 2023 under Macmillan's guidance, and has since become a reliable weapon.[^cite_note-TennisBiomech-522]

As Sabalenka's game has matured, she has also developed better shot selection and more variety. When she first arrived on tour, she was sometimes described as a player who hit everything as hard as she could. In the summer of 2018, her coach [Dmitry Tursunov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Tursunov) said her game was already evolving, and, "The major thing is she stopped trying \[to] hit a winner with every shot."[^cite_note-shot-selection2-526] Sabalenka has become increasingly willing to experiment with variety on her groundstrokes, and with finishing more points at the net.[^cite_note-527] During the 2024 clay season, she began to incorporate more [drop shots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drop_shot) into her game, which pair well with her power from the baseline.[^cite_note-528]

Sabalenka is primarily known for her success on [hardcourt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hardcourt). Nineteen of her twenty-two singles titles, including all four of her major titles, have been won on hardcourt, which suits her game due to its consistent bounce and fast-paced play. However, she is also recognised as a consistent contender in the late stages of [grass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grass_court) and [clay court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clay_court) tournaments. She has been particularly successful on faster clay courts, such as the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrid_Open_\(tennis\)), where she won the title in [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles), and [2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles).[^cite_note-WTAMadrid2021-126] She also reached her first major final on clay at the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) in 2025.

Sabalenka has become increasingly notable for her ability to win tiebreaks. In 2025, she set an [Open Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Era) record for most tiebreaks won by a woman in a single season, winning 22 out of 25 played.[^cite_note-WTAIW2026-452] This included a streak of nineteen consecutive tiebreaks won between February and October.[^cite_note-529] In 2026, Sabalenka won her twentieth consecutive tiebreak.[^cite_note-530] While Sabalenka is known for her clutch factor in tiebreaks, she has also lost a large number of closely-contested prominent matches, such as the [2025 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Women's_singles) final and [2026 Australian Open](81715708.md) final, and holds a 22–20 (52%) record in singles finals overall.

Sabalenka frequently accompanies her shots with loud [grunting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grunting_in_tennis). She said, "Honestly, I don't even hear myself when I am playing." She expressed her hope that her grunting does not disturb her opponents.[^cite_note-tennis-idols-531] At the [Australian Open](97851.md) in 2018, the crowd mocked her habit in a match against Ashleigh Barty.[^cite_note-grunting-33] She is an expressive personality on court.

## Coaches

Sabalenka had worked with Khalil Ibrahimov for two years up until early 2018. At this point, she began working with Swedish former professional tennis players [Magnus Norman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnus_Norman) and [Magnus Tideman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnus_Tideman).[^cite_note-start-norman-532][^cite_note-tideman-533] [Dmitry Tursunov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Tursunov) became her primary coach in time for the grass court season in 2018.[^cite_note-start-tursunov-534] Sabalenka briefly split with Tursunov after the 2019 US Open. Although they reunited later in the year, she made the split permanent at the end of the season. Sabalenka briefly worked with [Dieter Kindlmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dieter_Kindlmann) before switching coaches to her longtime hitting partner and compatriot [Anton Dubrov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anton_Dubrov), aged 25 at the time.[^cite_note-535][^cite_note-A24-536] In August 2022, Sabalenka hired biomechanics specialist Gavin MacMillan to improve on her serve.[^cite_note-537][^cite_note-538][^cite_note-539] In 2025, Belarusian former professional tennis player and 10-time [major](197638.md#Tournaments) doubles champion [Max Mirnyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Mirnyi) joined Sabalenka's coaching team as a consultant.[^cite_note-540]

As of 2025, her coaching team includes Dubrov; Mirnyi; fitness trainer Jason Stacy, a martial arts expert; and hitting partner [Andrei Vasilevski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrei_Vasilevski_\(tennis\)).[^cite_note-A24-536]

## Sponsorships



Sabalenka sponsored by [Nike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike,_Inc.) for sportswear and [Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilson_Sporting_Goods) for racquets

Sabalenka had been represented by [IMG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IMG_\(company\)). In January 2025, she departed from IMG and signed with Evolve, a company co-founded by [Naomi Osaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naomi_Osaka).[^cite_note-541]

In 2017, Sabalenka signed a sponsorship deal with [Nike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike,_Inc.).[^cite_note-542] Nike created a custom outfit for her for the [2023 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_US_Open_\(tennis\)).[^cite_note-543] She uses the [Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilson_Sporting_Goods) Blade 98 18x20 for her racquets.[^cite_note-544] She co-deigned the Wilson Blade 98 v9 Fighter Edition for her run at the [2025 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_US_Open_\(tennis\)).[^cite_note-545][^cite_note-546] In 2023, Sabalenka partnered with [Maestro Dobel Tequila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maestro_Dobel_Tequila).[^cite_note-547][^cite_note-548] In 2024, Sabalenka partnered with superfood brand Oakberry[^cite_note-549][^cite_note-550] and wearable technology brand [Whoop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whoop_\(company\)),[^cite_note-551] and joined [Audemars Piguet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audemars_Piguet) as a friend of the brand.[^cite_note-552] In 2025, Sabalenka became a global ambassador and minority shareholder of IM8, a health‑supplement brand co‑founded by former footballer [David Beckham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Beckham),[^cite_note-553][^cite_note-554] and partnered with hydration beverage brand Electrolit.[^cite_note-555]

## Personal life

Sabalenka has a tiger tattoo on her left arm that has earned her the nickname "The Tiger", which she has used to refer to herself.[^cite_note-556][^cite_note-557][^cite_note-wuhan-two-558] She studied at the [Belarusian State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarusian_State_University) in a sports-related program.[^cite_note-tut-2017-5] Her tennis idols growing up were [Serena Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serena_Williams) and [Maria Sharapova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Sharapova).[^cite_note-tennis-idols-531] Her father Sergey, a former ice hockey player, died suddenly in 2019 at age 43. He had [meningitis](21009963.md).[^cite_note-559][^cite_note-560]

Sabalenka's ex-boyfriend, [Konstantin Koltsov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konstantin_Koltsov), died of an apparent suicide in March 2024 at age 42.[^cite_note-561][^cite_note-562][^cite_note-563][^cite_note-564] She became engaged to Brazilian entrepreneur Georgios Frangulis on March 3, 2026.[^cite_note-565][^cite_note-566]

### Political views

In August 2020, during the [2020–2021 Belarusian protests](64399515.md), Sabalenka criticised the government of [Alexander Lukashenko](156589.md) for its dispersal of peaceful protests and called for no violence, but was criticised by members of the [Belarusian opposition](31371711.md) for not using her platform to advocate the protest movement's goals.[^cite_note-nn1-567][^cite_note-568] She separately praised the Belarusian government for "everything that is done for the country in general and for sports in particular".[^cite_note-nn1-567] In 2020, during widespread protests after the disputed election, Sabalenka signed an open letter that said that sport should remain outside of politics.[^cite_note-569][^cite_note-570] Belarusian media considered the letter supportive of Lukashenko, as it followed an open letter by Belarusian sporting figures demanding the election be invalidated.[^cite_note-571]

Following the start of the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine), Sabalenka expressed support for the Ukrainian people and the use of Ukraine-coloured ribbons: "I feel like people need our support. I just hope they understand that we're all really concerned. I think even 'sad' isn't even the right word."[^cite_note-572][^cite_note-573] In January 2023, with regard to the war and the banning of Russian and Belarusian players from the [2022 Wimbledon Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Wimbledon_Championships), Sabalenka said, "I just understand that it's not my fault."[^cite_note-574] Her comments on the Russian invasion of Ukraine and the situation of Ukrainian tennis players were criticised by [Lesia Tsurenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lesia_Tsurenko)'s coach Nikita Vlasov and former Ukrainian player [Sergiy Stakhovsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergiy_Stakhovsky) for lacking substance and compassion.[^cite_note-575][^cite_note-576] Sabalenka later indicated support for ending the war in Ukraine, saying: "If I could stop the war I would do it but unfortunately that doesn't lie in my hands. I just hope for peace."[^cite_note-577]

During a press conference at the [2023 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_French_Open), when asked about the war by Ukrainian journalist [Daria Meshcheriakova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daria_Meshcheriakova), she said: "Nobody in this world, Russian athletes or Belarusian athletes, support the war. Nobody. How can we support the war? Nobody, normal people will never support it."[^cite_note-ESPN2023-578] She later said: "I don't want my country to be in any conflict, I don't support war, meaning I don't support Lukashenko right now."[^cite_note-ESPN2023-578][^cite_note-579][^cite_note-580]

### Comments on transgender athletes

In December 2025, Sabalenka stated in an interview with [Piers Morgan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piers_Morgan) that she opposed the participation of [transgender women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender_women) in women's tennis. While saying she had "nothing against" transgender people, she argued that transgender women retained a biological advantage over female players and that it was unfair for women to compete against "biological men." She also said that female athletes train throughout their lives to reach the highest level and should not have to compete against opponents whom she believed possessed inherent physical advantages.[^cite_note-581]

In August 2026, Sabalenka reiterated her views on transgender athletes saying "it would be not really fair for the woman to compete against biological man" and voiced support for the WTA's new gender-testing policy that bans trans women from participating in the WTA Tour.[^cite_note-582]

### Television and film

Sabalenka appears in the tennis docuseries *[Break Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Break_Point_\(2023_TV_series\))*, which premiered on Netflix on 13 January 2023.[^cite_note-583]

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| 2026 | 1996 | Players to complete the [Sunshine Double](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunshine_Double) | [Steffi Graf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steffi_Graf), [Kim Clijsters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Clijsters), [Victoria Azarenka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Azarenka), [Iga Świątek](51838558.md) | [^cite_note-WTAMiami2026-461] |  |
| 2026 | 2026 | Players to complete the [Sunshine Double](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunshine_Double) in both singles (2026) and doubles (2019) | **Stands alone** | [^cite_note-Local10Miami-462] |  |
| 2026 | 1975 | Conceded 6–0 deciding sets at multiple and consecutive WTA-level events while ranked No. 1 | **Stands alone** | [^cite_note-587] |  |

| 2023-26 | 1969 | Most consecutive Grand Slam finals on hard court (8) | **Stands alone** | [^cite_note-589] |  |
| **[Australian Open](97851.md)** | 2024 | 1997 | Won the [Australian Open](97851.md) without dropping a set | [Ashleigh Barty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashleigh_Barty), [Serena Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serena_Williams), [Maria Sharapova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Sharapova), [Lindsay Davenport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lindsay_Davenport), [Martina Hingis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martina_Hingis) | [^cite_note-590][^cite_note-591] |

Беражыце сябе і сваіх блізкіх, мае дарагія беларусы». \[In August, Aryna Sabalenka condemned the dispersal of peaceful protests. "I can't believe that all this is happening in my peaceful native Belarus," the best Belarusian tennis player, the 11th ranked player in the world, wrote on her Instagram on August 13. – I can't sleep for the fourth night already. I can't stand the cruelty towards defenceless people. Why? Please stop the violence!!! Take care of yourself and your loved ones, my dear Belarusians."]
